## Q1

Biology · both · Molecular Basis of Inheritance

The final proof for DNA as the genetic material came from the experiments of

- (1) Hargobind Khorana
- (2) Griffith
- **(3)** Hershey and Chase
- (4) Avery, Mcleod and McCarty

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Hershey and Chase gave unequivocal proof which ended the debate between protein and DNA as genetic material.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Hershey and Chase (1952) grew bacteriophages with $^{32}\mathrm{P}$ (labels DNA) and $^{35}\mathrm{S}$ (labels protein); only $^{32}\mathrm{P}$ entered the bacteria, giving the unequivocal proof that DNA is the genetic material, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** DNA vs RNA & Genetic Material is DNA · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 2)

## Q2

Biology · both · Molecular Basis of Inheritance

Spliceosomes are not found in cells of

- **(1)** Bacteria
- (2) Plants
- (3) Fungi
- (4) Animals

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Spliceosomes are used in removal of introns during post-transcriptional processing of hnRNA in eukaryotes only as split genes are absent as prokaryotes.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Spliceosomes splice out introns from hnRNA, which happens only in eukaryotes with split genes (plants, fungi, animals). Bacteria are prokaryotes without split genes, so they have no spliceosomes, which is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Transcription · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 2)

![printed question 2](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/crops/q002_4876a8a4_30102026.png?v=4876a8a4bfa6)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 2](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/solutions/q002_daca7e62_30102026.png?v=daca7e62d7f9)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Spliceosomes carry out pre-mRNA splicing and exist only in eukaryotes; bacterial protein-coding genes carry no introns, so there is no splicing machinery
- **Trap:** **No trap** (`no_trap`) — nothing pulls; the other options are only filler  
  The three wrong options are Plants, Fungi and Animals. The single fact that decides the question, that splicing is a eukaryotic step, strikes all three out together, and no printed word in the stem favours one eukaryotic kingdom over the other two; even reading the stem without its 'not' leaves all three true at once rather than selecting one of them.
- **Why:** This question requires the one stated fact that splicing is confined to eukaryotes, demonstrated by the stem 'Spliceosomes are not found in cells of' standing over three options that are all eukaryotic kingdoms.
- **Note:** Three fillers is what this option set forces rather than padding: the key is the single prokaryote among four options, so the deciding fact removes the other three at the same moment and none of them can be singled out. Recorded as no_trap instead of fitting a mechanism to one eukaryote arbitrarily.

## Q3

Biology · zoology · Locomotion and Movement

The pivot joint between atlas and axis is a type of

- (1) Saddle joint
- (2) Fibrous joint
- (3) Cartilaginous joint
- **(4)** Synovial joint

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Synovial joints are freely movable joint which allow considerable movements.
2. Pivot joint is a type of synovial joint which provide rotational movement as in between atlas and axis vertebrae of vertebral column.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The pivot joint between atlas and axis allows rotation and has a fluid-filled synovial cavity between the bones, so it is a type of synovial joint, which is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Joints · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

## Q4

Biology · both · Molecular Basis of Inheritance

The association of histone H1 with a nucleosome indicates:

- (1) The DNA double helix is exposed
- (2) Transcription is occurring
- (3) DNA replication is occurring
- **(4)** The DNA is condensed into a Chromatin Fibre

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. The association of H1 protein indicates the complete formation of nucleosome.
2. Therefore the DNA is in condensed form.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Histone $H1$ is the linker histone: it binds the linker DNA between nucleosome cores, sealing the DNA wrapped around the histone octamer, and helps coil the beads-on-string into the chromatin fibre, so the DNA is condensed, which is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Packaging of DNA Helix · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 2)

![printed question 4](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/crops/q004_ffc188c0_30102026.png?v=ffc188c07df8)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 4](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/solutions/q004_3c1d9c4f_30102026.png?v=3c1d9c4f89e5)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Histone H1 binds the DNA where it enters and leaves the nucleosome and clamps nucleosomes together, so its presence marks DNA packed into the chromatin fibre
- **Trap:** **Reversed** (`reversed`) — the right thing stated backwards: direction, order or ratio — option 1  
  H1 is learnt by its position, bound outside the histone octamer on the stretch of DNA that enters and leaves the core, and 'outside' is read as 'exposed' -- the opposite of the packing state its binding marks.
- **Why:** This question requires the stated role of histone H1 in packaging, demonstrated by the key reading 'The DNA is condensed into a Chromatin Fibre' while option 1 states the opposite packing state and two further options name an activity instead of a state.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q5

Biology · botany · Anatomy of Flowering Plants

Which of the following is made up of dead cells?

- (1) Phloem
- (2) Xylem parenchyma
- (3) Collenchyma
- **(4)** Phellem

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Cork cambium undergoes periclinal division and cuts off thick walled suberised dead cork cells towards outside and it cuts off thin walled living cells i.e., phelloderm on inner side.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Phloem is mostly living: sieve tube elements, companion cells and phloem parenchyma are living, and only the phloem fibres are dead, so option $1$ is not made up of dead cells.
2. Xylem parenchyma is the living part of xylem, so option $2$ is out.
3. Collenchyma is a simple tissue of living cells with cellulose and pectin thickenings at the corners, so option $3$ is out.
4. Phellem (cork) cells are dead at maturity, with suberin deposited in their walls, so the answer is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Secondary Growth · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 2)

![printed question 5](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/crops/q005_dabe1b11_30102026.png?v=dabe1b118b38)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 5](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/solutions/q005_4641ce59_30102026.png?v=4641ce594f2a)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Phellem (cork) is dead and suberised at maturity, while xylem parenchyma, collenchyma and the conducting cells of phloem are living
- **Trap:** **Neighbouring fact** (`nearby_fact`) — a true fact about a neighbouring thing is offered instead — option 2  
  'Xylem' is stored together with 'dead cells' because the tracheids, vessels and fibres printed beside it are dead at maturity, and the one living element of the xylem is the parenchyma this option names.
- **Why:** This question requires which named tissue the source describes as dead at maturity, demonstrated by the stem 'Which of the following is made up of dead cells?' and by the option 'Xylem parenchyma' naming one element rather than the tissue. Trap test applied: replacing 'xylem parenchyma' and 'phellem' with two unlike words leaves the pull intact, because it rides on the dead-cell fact printed for the neighbouring xylem elements, so nearby_fact and not look_alike_term.

## Q6

Biology · zoology · Structural Organisation in Animals

Select the correct route for the passage of sperms in male frogs :

- **(1)** Testes $\rightarrow$ Vasa efferentia $\rightarrow$ Kidney $\rightarrow$ Bidder's canal $\rightarrow$ Urinogenital duct $\rightarrow$ Cloaca
- (2) Testes $\rightarrow$ Bidder's canal $\rightarrow$ Kidney $\rightarrow$ Vasa efferentia $\rightarrow$ Urinogenital duct $\rightarrow$ Cloaca
- (3) Testes $\rightarrow$ Vasa efferentia $\rightarrow$ Kidney $\rightarrow$ Seminal Vesicle $\rightarrow$ Urinogenital duct $\rightarrow$ Cloaca
- (4) Testes $\rightarrow$ Vasa efferentia $\rightarrow$ Bidder's canal $\rightarrow$ Ureter $\rightarrow$ Cloaca

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. In male frog the sperms will move from Testes $\rightarrow$ Vasa efferentia $\rightarrow$ Kidney $\rightarrow$ Bidder’s canal $\rightarrow$ Urinogenital duct $\rightarrow$ Cloaca.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. As NCERT Class 11 describes the male frog, the testes give off vasa efferentia, which enter the kidney, open into Bidder's canal, and Bidder's canal joins the urinogenital duct that opens into the cloaca, so the route $\text{Testes} \rightarrow \text{Vasa efferentia} \rightarrow \text{Kidney} \rightarrow \text{Bidder's canal} \rightarrow \text{Urinogenital duct} \rightarrow \text{Cloaca}$ is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Anatomy of Frog · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 2)

![printed question 6](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/crops/q006_a01ee30e_30102026.png?v=a01ee30e19f0)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

- **Concept:** In the male frog sperm leave the testes by the vasa efferentia into the kidney, pass through Bidder's canal into the urinogenital duct and reach the cloaca

## Q7

Biology · zoology · Body Fluids and Circulation

Adult human RBCs are enucleate. Which of the following statement(s) is/are most appropriate explanation for this feature?

- **(a)** They do not need to reproduce
- **(b)** They are somatic cells
- **(c)** They do not metabolize
- **(d)** All their internal space is available for oxygen transport

- (1) (b) and (c)
- **(2)** Only (d)
- (3) Only (a)
- (4) (a), (c) and (d)

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. In Human RBCs, nucleus degenerates during maturation which provide more space for oxygen carrying pigment (Haemoglobin).
2. It lacks most of the cell organelles including mitochondria so respires anaerobically.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Statement $(a)$: not needing to reproduce does not explain loss of the nucleus, since many non-dividing cells (e.g. neurons) keep their nuclei, so $(a)$ is not the appropriate explanation.
2. Statement $(b)$: almost all somatic cells are nucleated, so being somatic does not explain enucleation; $(b)$ is rejected.
3. Statement $(c)$: mature RBCs do metabolise (they make ATP by anaerobic glycolysis), so $(c)$ is false.
4. Statement $(d)$: without a nucleus (and mitochondria) the whole internal space is filled with haemoglobin, maximising $O_2$ transport, so $(d)$ is the appropriate explanation.
5. Only $(d)$ is correct, which is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Formed Elements And Their Types · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 2)

![printed question 7](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/crops/q007_d03f1f49_30102026.png?v=d03f1f49e3c8)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 7](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/solutions/q007_f303db78_30102026.png?v=f303db784d26)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **Statements** (`statements`) — lettered statements, the answer is which combination is right
- **Asks:** **Stated reason** (`reason_line`) — a reason NCERT gives: why it happens
- **Concept:** The mammalian red cell gives up its nucleus so that the whole of its interior is free to be packed with haemoglobin for carrying oxygen
- **Trap:** **Partly correct** (`half_right`) — one part of the option is right and one part is wrong — option 4  
  A student who has settled on (d) and then reads the task as collecting everything that sounds true adds (a) and the false (c), and a cell that does no metabolism could not keep its own membrane or its haemoglobin in order.
- **Why:** This question requires the reason behind a stated feature, demonstrated by the stem 'Adult human RBCs are enucleate. Which of the following statement(s) is/are most appropriate explanation for this feature?'.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q8

Biology · zoology · Strategies for Enhancement in Food Production

Homozygous purelines in cattle can be obtained by

- (1) Mating of individuals of different species
- **(2)** Mating of related individuals of same breed
- (3) Mating of unrelated individuals of same breed
- (4) Mating of individuals of different breed

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Inbreeding results in increase in the homozygosity.
2. Therefore, mating of the related individuals of same breed will increase homozygosity.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Inbreeding, the mating of more closely related individuals within the same breed for $4$-$6$ generations, increases homozygosity, so it is how purelines are developed in cattle; this is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Animal Breeding · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 2)

![printed question 8](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/crops/q008_20f89b52_30102026.png?v=20f89b52af48)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 8](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/solutions/q008_6e495af7_30102026.png?v=6e495af7fdae)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Inbreeding - mating closely related animals within one breed - is what raises homozygosity and fixes a pureline, while every form of out-breeding does the opposite.
- **Trap:** **Neighbouring fact** (`nearby_fact`) — a true fact about a neighbouring thing is offered instead — option 3  
  That sentence is the printed definition of out-crossing, which sits in the out-breeding subsection right beside inbreeding and keeps the words 'same breed' that the key also carries, so a student holding the breed clause but not the relatedness clause ticks it. Out-crossing is used to break homozygosity, not to build it.
- **Why:** This question requires the stated breeding method that raises homozygosity, demonstrated by four options differing only in relatedness and breed - 'Mating of related individuals of same breed' against 'Mating of unrelated individuals of same breed'.
- **Note:** E1's pattern returned statement_options on the length of the options; no statement block is printed and the stem is an incomplete sentence each option completes, so the shape recorded is mcq.

## Q9

Biology · zoology · Chemical Coordination and Integration

A temporary endocrine gland in the human body is

- (1) Corpus allatum
- (2) Pineal gland
- (3) Corpus cardiacum
- **(4)** Corpus luteum

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Corpus luteum is the temporary endocrine structure formed in the ovary after ovulation.
2. It is responsible for the release of the hormones like progesterone, oestrogen etc.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The corpus luteum forms from the ruptured Graafian follicle after ovulation, secretes progesterone, and degenerates when there is no fertilisation. This makes it a temporary endocrine gland in humans. The corpus allatum and corpus cardiacum are insect glands, and the pineal gland is permanent, so the answer is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Gonads · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 2)

![printed question 9](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/crops/q009_daf5889d_30102026.png?v=daf5889deea0)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 9](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/solutions/q009_fd3ded26_30102026.png?v=fd3ded262f11)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** The corpus luteum forms from the ruptured follicle after ovulation and regresses when pregnancy does not follow, which makes it the one temporary endocrine gland in the human body.
- **Trap:** **Condition dropped** (`condition_missed`) — drop the condition the stem states and the option becomes true — option 2  
  Delete the word 'temporary' from the stem and the pineal gland answers it - it is a human endocrine gland, and it is the only option besides the key that is human at all. The pineal is present from birth to death, so the deleted word is the whole question.
- **Why:** This question requires the one gland satisfying both printed conditions, demonstrated by the stem's 'temporary' and 'in the human body' against options of which only one meets both.
- **Note:** look_alike_term was weighed for options 1 and 3, which share the word 'Corpus' with the key. condition_missed is recorded as the dominant trap because the one option that is true of the human body fails on nothing but the stem's printed word 'temporary' - delete it and option 2 answers the question as written.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q10

Biology · botany · Biological Classification

Viroids differ from viruses in having :

- **(1)** RNA molecules without protein coat
- (2) DNA molecules with protein coat
- (3) DNA molecules without protein coat
- (4) RNA molecules with protein coat

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Viroids are sub-viral agents as infectious RNA particles, without protein coat.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Viroids are infectious agents made of free RNA only, with no protein coat, unlike viruses which have a protein capsid around their nucleic acid; this matches option $1$.

## Q11

Biology · zoology · Excretory Products and Their Elimination

A decrease in blood pressure/volume will not cause the release of

- (1) ADH
- (2) Renin
- **(3)** Atrial Natriuretic Factor
- (4) Aldosterone

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. A decrease in blood pressure / volume stimulates the release of renin, aldosterone, and ADH while increase in blood pressure / volume stimulates the release of Atrial Natriuretic Factor (ANF) which cause vasodilation and also inhibits RAAS (Renin Angiotensin Aldosterone System) mechanism that decreases the blood volume/pressure.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. A fall in blood pressure or volume makes the juxtaglomerular cells release renin, which drives the renin–angiotensin–aldosterone system, so $\text{Renin}$ (option $2$) and $\text{Aldosterone}$ (option $4$) are released.
2. A fall in blood volume also triggers the hypothalamus–neurohypophysis to release $\text{ADH}$ (option $1$) to conserve water.
3. Atrial natriuretic factor is released by the atrial walls only when blood pressure/volume rises, and it opposes the RAAS, so a fall does not release it: option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Regulation Of Kidney Function · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 3)

![printed question 11](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/crops/q011_498353e9_30102026.png?v=498353e9ce06)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 11](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/solutions/q011_4b26e0be_30102026.png?v=4b26e0be3248)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated reason** (`reason_line`) — a reason NCERT gives: why it happens
- **Concept:** A fall in blood pressure or volume releases renin, then angiotensin and aldosterone, and also ADH; ANF is the opposite signal, released when a rise in blood volume stretches the atrial wall.
- **Trap:** **Condition dropped** (`condition_missed`) — drop the condition the stem states and the option becomes true — option 2  
  Renin is the hormone printed against a fall in glomerular blood pressure, so the stem's opening words point straight at it. Deleting the 'not' from the stem is all it takes to make renin the right answer, and that is the step the option rewards.
- **Why:** This question requires the one hormone whose release a stated change does not trigger, demonstrated by the stem 'A decrease in blood pressure/volume will not cause the release of' against three hormones the same decrease does release.
- **Note:** asks is recorded as reason_line rather than fact_line because the stem asks for the outcome of a stated change rather than an isolated name; pick_cause_or_effect maps to reason_line in category_retired.asks.
<!--/ecat-->

- **Concept:** In coconut the endosperm itself is what is eaten - liquid as coconut water and cellular as the white kernel.

## Q15

Biology · zoology · Reproductive Health

Match the following sexually transmitted diseases (Column - I) with their causative agent (Column - II) and select the correct option.

| List-I | | List-II | |
|---|---|---|---|
| (a) | Gonorrhea | (i) | HIV |
| (b) | Syphilis | (ii) | Neisseria |
| (c) | Genital Warts | (iii) | Treponema |
| (d) | AIDS | (iv) | Human Papilloma virus |

- (1) (iv) (iii) (ii) (i)
- **(2)** (ii) (iii) (iv) (i)
- (3) (iii) (iv) (i) (ii)
- (4) (iv) (ii) (iii) (i)

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Gonorrhoea – Neisseria (Bacteria)
2. Syphilis – Treponema (Bacteria)
3. Genital Warts – Human papilloma virus (Virus)
4. AIDS – HIV (Virus)

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Gonorrhea is caused by $Neisseria$ (gonorrhoeae), so (a) $\rightarrow$ (ii).
2. Syphilis is caused by $Treponema$ (pallidum), so (b) $\rightarrow$ (iii).
3. Genital warts are caused by Human Papilloma virus, so (c) $\rightarrow$ (iv).
4. AIDS is caused by HIV, so (d) $\rightarrow$ (i).
5. The sequence (ii) (iii) (iv) (i) matches option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Sexually Transmitted Infections (STIs) · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 3)

![printed question 15](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/crops/q015_ee2df905_30102026.png?v=ee2df9058470)

- **Concept:** Each sexually transmitted infection carries one named causative agent: gonorrhoea from Neisseria, syphilis from Treponema, genital warts from the human papilloma virus and AIDS from HIV
- **Trap:** **Neighbouring fact** (`nearby_fact`) — a true fact about a neighbouring thing is offered instead — option 1  
  Syphilis-Treponema and AIDS-HIV are the two pairs the source prints most plainly, and option 1 keeps both; the two agents left over are then assigned the wrong way round, so gonorrhoea takes the papilloma virus and genital warts take Neisseria.
- **Why:** This question requires the causative agent the source names for each listed infection, demonstrated by the stem's instruction to match the diseases of Column I 'with their causative agent (Column - II)' and by Column II holding nothing but agent names.

## Q19

Biology · both · Molecular Basis of Inheritance

During DNA replication, Okazaki fragments are used to enlongate

- **(1)** The lagging strand away from the replication fork
- (2) The leading strand towards replication fork
- (3) The lagging strand towards replication fork
- (4) The leading strand away from replication fork

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Two DNA polymerase molecules work simultaneous at the DNA fork, one on the leading strand and the other on the lagging strand.
2. Each Okazaki fragment is synthesized by DNA polymerase at lagging strand in $5' \rightarrow 3'$ direction.
3. New Okazaki fragments appear as the replication fork opens further.
4. As the first Okazaki fragment appears away from the replication fork, the direction of elongation would be away from replication fork.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. DNA polymerase adds nucleotides only in the $5' \rightarrow 3'$ direction, so only one strand (the leading strand) can be made continuously towards the replication fork.
2. The other strand (the lagging strand) is made discontinuously as short Okazaki fragments, so options $2$ and $4$ (leading strand) are ruled out.
3. Each Okazaki fragment is synthesised $5' \rightarrow 3'$, which on the lagging strand runs away from the replication fork; the fragments are then joined by DNA ligase.
4. So Okazaki fragments elongate the lagging strand away from the replication fork, which is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** The Machinery and the Enzymes of Replication · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 4)

![printed question 19](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/crops/q019_3b5251bc_30102026.png?v=3b5251bce37e)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 19](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/solutions/q019_88691ab9_30102026.png?v=88691ab949f5)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Rule applied** (`apply_rule`) — a rule you already know, used on a case you have not seen
- **Concept:** Okazaki fragments are the short pieces of the discontinuously built lagging strand; because every piece is built 5' to 3', each one runs away from the advancing replication fork.
- **Trap:** **Reversed** (`reversed`) — the right thing stated backwards: direction, order or ratio — option 3  
  Knowing the fragments belong to the lagging strand settles the strand but not the direction, and the fork is where the action is, so 'towards' is the half that gets filled in.
- **Why:** This question requires the 5' to 3' polarity rule applied to the lagging template, demonstrated by the four options crossing 'lagging' against 'leading' with 'towards' against 'away from the replication fork'.
- **Note:** E1 called this statement_options; the options are completions of the stem's own sentence rather than statements judged for truth, so the shape on the page is a plain mcq with a trailing blank. The stem prints 'enlongate' for 'elongate'.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q20

Biology · botany · Organisms and Populations

Mycorrhizae are the example of

- **(1)** Mutualism
- (2) Fungistasis
- (3) Amensalism
- (4) Antibiosis

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Mycorrhizae is a symbiotic association of fungi with roots of higher plants.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Mycorrhiza is an association of a fungus with the roots of a higher plant, in which the fungus helps absorb nutrients such as phosphorus and the plant supplies energy-yielding carbohydrates to the fungus; since both partners benefit $(+,+)$, it is mutualism, option $1$.

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Mycorrhiza is a plus-plus association: the fungus takes sugars from the root and the root gains phosphorus and water uptake, which is what mutualism means.
- **Trap:** **Neighbouring fact** (`nearby_fact`) — a true fact about a neighbouring thing is offered instead — option 3  
  Remembering only that the fungus gains from the association files it in the row where a single partner's sign is positive, amensalism, instead of the plus-plus row.

## Q21

## Q23

Biology · botany · Biological Classification

Which among the following are the smallest living cells, known without a definite cell wall, pathogenic to plants as well as animals and can survive without oxygen?

- (1) Nostoc
- (2) Bacillus
- (3) Pseudomonas
- **(4)** Mycoplasma

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Mycoplasmas are smallest, wall-less prokaryotes, pleomorphic in nature.
2. These are pathogenic on both plants and animals.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Organisms that completely lack a cell wall, are the smallest living cells known, can survive without oxygen and cause disease in plants and animals are $\text{Mycoplasma}$, which is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Archaebacteria & Mycoplasma · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

## Q24

Which of the following components provides sticky character to the bacterial cell?

1. Sticky character of the bacterial wall is due to glycocalyx or slime layer.
2. This layer is rich in glycoproteins.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. In NCERT's account of the prokaryotic cell envelope, the outermost layer is the glycocalyx (a loose slime layer or a thick, tough capsule), and it is this layer that makes the bacterial cell sticky, so the answer is option $1$ (Glycocalyx).

## Q25

Biology · botany · Photosynthesis in Higher Plants

With reference to factors affecting the rate of photosynthesis, which of the following statements is not correct?

- (1) Tomato is a greenhouse crop which can be grown in $\mathrm{CO_2}$ - enriched atmosphere for higher yield
- (2) Light saturation for $\mathrm{CO_2}$ fixation occurs at $10\%$ of full sunlight
- (3) Increasing atmospheric $\mathrm{CO_2}$ concentration upto $0.05\%$ can enhance $\mathrm{CO_2}$ fixation rate
- **(4)** $\mathrm{C_3}$ plants responds to higher temperatures with enhanced photosynthesis while $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants have much lower temperature optimum

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. In $\mathrm{C_3}$ plants photosynthesis is decreased at higher temperature due to increased photorespiration.
2. $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants have higher temperature optimum because of the presence of pyruvate phosphate dikinase enzyme, which is sensitive to low temperature.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Option $1$ is correct: tomato and bell pepper are greenhouse crops grown in $\mathrm{CO_2}$-enriched air for higher yield.
2. Option $2$ is correct: light saturation occurs at about $10\%$ of full sunlight, so light is rarely limiting except for shaded plants.
3. Option $3$ is correct: raising $\mathrm{CO_2}$ up to about $0.05\%$ increases the $\mathrm{CO_2}$ fixation rate.
4. Option $4$ is wrong: $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants show higher photosynthesis at higher temperatures, while $\mathrm{C_3}$ plants have a much lower temperature optimum, so the statement swaps $\mathrm{C_3}$ and $\mathrm{C_4}$.
5. The statement that is not correct is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Factors Affecting Photosynthesis · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 4)

![printed question 25](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/crops/q025_6db5ce16_30102026.png?v=6db5ce161290)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 25](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/solutions/q025_cf536778_30102026.png?v=cf536778b386)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **Statements** (`statements`) — lettered statements, the answer is which combination is right
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** C4 plants have the higher temperature optimum and respond to higher temperatures with more photosynthesis, while C3 plants peak lower; the other printed facts — light saturation at 10 per cent of full sunlight and CO2 enrichment of greenhouse crops — stand as stated
- **Trap:** **Answers another question** (`answers_other_question`) — true, and about this topic, but it answers something else — option 2  
  The figure "10% of full sunlight" reads as far too low for saturation, so the statement a student doubts is flagged as the incorrect one, when the statement is true as printed and the false one is the C3/C4 temperature claim
- **Why:** This question requires the stated facts about light, CO2 and temperature as factors, demonstrated by the stem's "which of the following statements is not correct?" against option 4's claim that "$\mathrm{C_3}$ plants responds to higher temperatures with enhanced photosynthesis while $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants have much lower temperature optimum".
- **Note:** The keyed option is itself a reversal of the printed C3/C4 comparison, but a reversal in the ANSWER is not a trap; the trap axis records what pulls a student off the key, which here is a true-as-printed statement being flagged because its number reads as implausible.

## Q26

Biology · zoology · Digestion and Absorption

Which of the following options best represents the enzyme composition of pancreatic juice?

- **(1)** Lipase, amylase, trypsinogen, procarboxypeptidase
- (2) Amylase, peptidase, trypsinogen, rennin
- (3) Amylase, pepsin, trypsinogen, maltase
- (4) Peptidase, amylase, pepsin, rennin

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Rennin and Pepsin enzymes are present in the gastric juice.
2. Maltase is present in the intestinal juice.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. NCERT lists pancreatic juice enzymes as trypsinogen, chymotrypsinogen, procarboxypeptidases, amylases, lipases and nucleases; pepsin and rennin are gastric enzymes and maltase is an intestinal enzyme, so only option $1$ (lipase, amylase, trypsinogen, procarboxypeptidase) fits.

## Q27

Biology · both · Biomolecules

Which one of the following statements is correct, with reference to enzymes?

- (1) Holoenzyme = Coenzyme + Cofactor
- (2) Apoenzyme = Holoenzyme + Coenzyme
- **(3)** Holoenzyme = Apoenzyme + Coenzyme
- (4) Coenzyme = Apoenzyme + Holoenzyme

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Holoenzyme is conjugated enzyme in which protein part is apoenzyme while non-protein is cofactor.
2. Coenzyme are also organic compounds but their association with apoenzyme is only transient and serve as cofactors.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The catalytically active complete enzyme is the $\text{holoenzyme}$; its protein part alone is the $\text{apoenzyme}$ and an organic non-protein cofactor is a $\text{coenzyme}$, so $\text{Holoenzyme} = \text{Apoenzyme} + \text{Coenzyme}$, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Classification And Nomenclature Of Enzymes And Cofactors · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 5)

![printed question 27](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/crops/q027_b2fed981_30102026.png?v=b2fed9814f8a)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 27](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/solutions/q027_ffdf85ae_30102026.png?v=ffdf85ae76d7)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **Statements** (`statements`) — lettered statements, the answer is which combination is right
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** An enzyme's protein part is the apoenzyme and the non-protein helper is the coenzyme; together they make the complete, active holoenzyme
- **Trap:** **look alike term** (`look_alike_term`) — two terms RESEMBLE each other and the resemblance is the pull: maturation / meristematic, lyase / ligase, GPP / NPP — option 1  
  Coenzyme and cofactor read as two names for the same non-protein helper, so an equation built from both "co-" words looks like the definition of the complete enzyme
- **Why:** This question requires the stated relation between an enzyme's named parts, demonstrated by the stem asking which statement "is correct, with reference to enzymes" against option 3's "Holoenzyme = Apoenzyme + Coenzyme".

## Q28

Biology · both · Molecular Basis of Inheritance

If there are 999 bases in an RNA that codes for a protein with 333 amino acids, and the base at position 901 is deleted such that the length of the RNA becomes 998 bases, how many codons will be altered?

- (1) 333
- (2) 1
- (3) 11
- **(4)** 33

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. If deletion occurs at $901^{\mathrm{st}}$ position the remaining $98$ bases specifying for $33$ codons of amino acids will be altered.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The RNA has $\frac{999}{3} = 333$ codons, one per amino acid.
2. Codon $n$ spans bases $3n-2$ to $3n$, so base $901 = 3(301) - 2$ is the first base of codon $301$.
3. Codons $1$ to $300$ (bases $1$ to $900$) lie before the deletion and stay unchanged.
4. Deleting base $901$ causes a frameshift, so every codon from $301$ to $333$ is read differently.
5. Altered codons $= 333 - 301 + 1 = 33$, which is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Effect of Mutation on Genetic Code · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 5)

![printed question 28](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/crops/q028_f5e39637_30102026.png?v=f5e39637aaab)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 28](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/solutions/q028_903b17af_30102026.png?v=903b17afaff3)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Calculation** (`calculate`) — put numbers in and get a number out
- **Concept:** A single base deletion shifts the reading frame from that point on, so every codon downstream of the deletion is altered and the codons before it are untouched
- **Trap:** **Condition dropped** (`condition_missed`) — drop the condition the stem states and the option becomes true — option 1  
  The frameshift rule is applied without the position the stem prints: "a deletion alters every codon after it" becomes "a deletion alters every codon", and all 333 are counted
- **Why:** This question requires counting the codons downstream of a stated deletion point, demonstrated by the stem's "the base at position 901 is deleted such that the length of the RNA becomes 998 bases, how many codons will be altered?".
<!--/ecat-->

## Q29

Biology · botany · Organisms and Populations

Asymptote in a logistic growth curve is obtained when

- (1) $K < N$
- (2) The value of 'r' approaches zero
- **(3)** $K = N$
- (4) $K > N$

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. A population growing in a habitat with limited resources shows logistic growth curve.
2. For logistic growth
3. $\frac{dN}{dt} = rN\left(\frac{K - N}{K}\right)$
4. If $K = N$ then $\frac{K - N}{K} = 0$
5. $\therefore$ the $\frac{dN}{dt} = 0$,
6. the population reaches asymptote.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Logistic growth follows $\frac{dN}{dt} = rN\left(\frac{K-N}{K}\right)$.
2. When $N = K$, $\frac{K-N}{K} = 0$, so $\frac{dN}{dt} = 0$ and the curve flattens into the asymptote; this is option $3$ ($K = N$).

**Subtopic:** Logistic Growth · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 5)

![printed question 29](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/crops/q029_35b6a85e_30102026.png?v=35b6a85efd45)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 29](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/solutions/q029_1b510786_30102026.png?v=1b510786a893)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** The logistic curve flattens into its asymptote when the population reaches the carrying capacity of the habitat, that is when N equals K
- **Trap:** **Answers another question** (`answers_other_question`) — true, and about this topic, but it answers something else — option 4  
  An asymptote is approached and never touched, so equality is rejected and the inequality that holds all the way up the rising part of the sigmoid, $K>N$, is taken for the plateau condition
- **Why:** This question requires the stated condition under which logistic growth levels off, demonstrated by the stem's "Asymptote in a logistic growth curve is obtained when" with carrying capacity and population size offered as the three possible inequalities.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q30

Biology · botany · Plant Kingdom

Select the mismatch :

- (1) Equisetum – Homosporous
- **(2)** Pinus – Dioecious
- (3) Cycas – Dioecious
- (4) Salvinia – Heterosporous

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Pinus is monoecious plant having both male and female cones on same plant.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. $\text{Equisetum}$ (a pteridophyte) bears one kind of spore, so it is homosporous: option $1$ is a correct match.
2. $\text{Pinus}$ bears male and female cones on the same plant, so it is monoecious, not dioecious: option $2$ is a mismatch.
3. $\text{Cycas}$ bears male cones and megasporophylls on separate plants, so it is dioecious: option $3$ is a correct match.
4. $\text{Salvinia}$ produces microspores and megaspores, so it is heterosporous: option $4$ is a correct match.
5. The only mismatch is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Gymnosperms · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 5)

![printed question 30](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/crops/q030_414e4c88_30102026.png?v=414e4c880bd9)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 30](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/solutions/q030_5a2803fa_30102026.png?v=5a2803fa6d2a)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **Two answers** (`two_part`) — the answer has two halves, usually joined by 'respectively'
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Pinus bears male and female cones on the same plant and so is monoecious, while Cycas carries them on separate plants and is dioecious; Equisetum is homosporous and Salvinia heterosporous
- **Trap:** **Reversed** (`reversed`) — the right thing stated backwards: direction, order or ratio — option 3  
  The dioecious-monoecious assignment between the two gymnosperms is held the wrong way round, so the student who knows one of the pair is mislabelled strikes out Cycas instead of Pinus
- **Why:** This question requires the stated reproductive trait of each named plant, demonstrated by the instruction "Select the mismatch" against the pairing "Pinus – Dioecious".
- **Note:** E1 recorded plain_mcq, but each option prints an organism paired with a trait and the pairing is accepted or rejected whole, so the printed shape has two parts per option and two_part is recorded; the current qtype vocabulary has no pair value. nearby_fact was considered for option 3 and rejected: Cycas and Pinus are not alike as names, and what carries the student is holding the assignment between them backwards rather than the position of either in the text.

## Q31

Biology · both · Cell Cycle and Cell Division

Anaphase promoting complex (APC) is a protein degradation machinery necessary for proper mitosis of animal cells. If APC is defective in a human cell, which of the following is expected to occur?

- (1) Recombination of chromosome arms will occur
- (2) Chromosomes will not condense
- (3) Chromosomes will be fragmented
- **(4)** Chromosomes will not segregate

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Anaphase Promoting Complex (APC) is a protein necessary for separation of daughter chromosomes during anaphase.
2. If APC is defective then the chromosomes will fail to segregate during anaphase.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. APC is a ubiquitin ligase whose action at the metaphase-to-anaphase transition degrades securin, freeing separase to cleave cohesin that holds sister chromatids together; a defective APC means cohesin is not cleaved, sister chromatids cannot separate at anaphase, and chromosomes will not segregate, which is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Phases of Cell Cycle · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 5)

![printed question 31](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/crops/q031_396a4053_30102026.png?v=396a40538579)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 31](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/solutions/q031_62b6b604_30102026.png?v=62b6b6043f98)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Rule applied** (`apply_rule`) — a rule you already know, used on a case you have not seen
- **Concept:** Anaphase is the stage at which sister chromatids are pulled apart, so a machinery named for promoting anaphase is needed for chromosome segregation and nothing else in mitosis
- **Trap:** **Wrong site attacked** (`wrong_site_attacked`) — the reagent is right but it attacks the other site, atom or bond than the one the question turns on — option 2  
  The failure is placed at the wrong stage of mitosis: the stem says the complex is "necessary for proper mitosis", and condensation is the mitotic chromosome event most firmly remembered, so the defect is expected to show up there
- **Why:** This question requires a stage of mitosis to be identified from what a supplied complex does, demonstrated by the stem defining APC as "a protein degradation machinery necessary for proper mitosis" and asking "which of the following is expected to occur?".
- **Note:** E1 recorded statement_options because the options are full sentences, but the stem does not ask for statements to be judged — it asks which outcome follows — so qtype is mcq. The NOT in negative_words_seen comes from the option wording "will not segregate" and "will not condense", not from the task.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q32

Biology · botany · Ecosystem

Which ecosystem has the maximum biomass?

- (1) Lake ecosystem
- **(2)** Forest ecosystem
- (3) Grassland ecosystem
- (4) Pond ecosystem

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. High productive ecosystem are
2. – Tropical rain forest
3. – Coral reef
4. – Estuaries
5. – Sugarcane fields

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Biomass here is the standing crop, i.e. the mass of living matter per unit area; forest producers (trees) accumulate large woody biomass, while grassland herbs and aquatic phytoplankton in lakes and ponds keep a small standing crop, so the forest ecosystem has the maximum biomass, which is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Types of Ecosystems · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 5)

![printed question 32](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/crops/q032_86cc585a_30102026.png?v=86cc585afb19)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Standing biomass is the mass of living material present at a moment, and a forest holds the most of it because trunks, branches and roots accumulate as wood
- **Trap:** **Answers another question** (`answers_other_question`) — true, and about this topic, but it answers something else — option 3  
  "Maximum biomass" is read as "most growth": grassland is the system remembered for fast-growing, quickly regrowing vegetation, so the option answers for the rate at which material is produced rather than for the stock standing in the ecosystem
- **Why:** This question requires the stated ranking of ecosystems by standing biomass, demonstrated by the stem's "Which ecosystem has the maximum biomass?" with forest, grassland and two aquatic systems as the options.
- **Note:** The distractors here are weak: only the grassland option has a route, and it rests on biomass being swapped for productivity. No frequency claim is made for it.

## Q33

Biology · botany · Plant Kingdom

Zygotic meiosis is characterstic of

- **(1)** Chlamydomonas
- (2) Marchantia
- (3) Fucus
- (4) Funaria

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Chlamydomonas has haplontic life cycle hence showing zygotic meiosis or initial meiosis.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Zygotic meiosis means the diploid zygote divides by meiosis, which is the haplontic life cycle; Chlamydomonas is haplontic, Fucus is diplontic (gametic meiosis) and Marchantia and Funaria are haplo-diplontic bryophytes (sporic meiosis), so the answer is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Plant Life Cycles and Alternation of Generations · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

- **Concept:** In a haplontic life cycle the zygote is the only diploid cell and divides by meiosis at once, so the plant body stays haploid
- **Trap:** **Neighbouring fact** (`nearby_fact`) — a true fact about a neighbouring thing is offered instead — option 3  
  Chlamydomonas and Fucus are printed in the same NCERT paragraph as the two extreme life cycles, so a student who holds that meiosis sits at one point of the cycle but not which point takes the diplontic alga. In Fucus the thallus is diploid and the only haploid cells are the gametes, so meiosis happens at gamete formation, not in the zygote.
- **Why:** This question requires the life-cycle pattern NCERT assigns to each named plant, demonstrated by 'Zygotic meiosis is characterstic of' followed by four bare organism names with no criterion printed to apply.
- **Note:** nearby_fact over look_alike_term by the swap test: Fucus and Chlamydomonas are not alike as words, and replacing Fucus with another named alga leaves the pull intact, so position in the printed life-cycle paragraph is doing the work.

## Q34

Biology · zoology · Chemical Coordination and Integration

Hypersecretion of Growth Hormone in adults does not cause further increase in height, because

- (1) Muscle fibres do not grow in size after birth
- (2) Growth Hormone becomes inactive in adults
- **(3)** Epiphyseal plates close after adolescence
- (4) Bones loose their sensitivity to Growth Hormone in adults

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Epiphyseal plate is responsible for the growth of bone which close after adolescence so hypersecretion of growth hormone in adults does not cause further increase in height.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Height increases only by longitudinal bone growth at the epiphyseal (growth) plates; these fuse after adolescence, so excess growth hormone in adults cannot increase height (it causes acromegaly instead), which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** The Pituitary Gland · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 5)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated reason** (`reason_line`) — a reason NCERT gives: why it happens
- **Concept:** Growth hormone can lengthen a long bone only while its epiphyseal plate is open; once the plate fuses after adolescence the extra hormone thickens bone instead of adding height
- **Trap:** **Wrong site attacked** (`wrong_site_attacked`) — the reagent is right but it attacks the other site, atom or bond than the one the question turns on — option 4  
  A student who knows the hormone is still secreted and still working in adults looks for the block in the target, and 'bones loose their sensitivity' is the only target-side reason printed. Adult bone does still answer growth hormone, which is why acromegaly thickens jaw, hands and feet; what is gone is the epiphyseal cartilage plate, so the block sits at the plate and not at the bone's response.
- **Why:** This question requires the reason NCERT gives for growth hormone failing to add height after adolescence, demonstrated by the stem ending 'does not cause further increase in height, because' with each option a rival cause.

## Q35

Biology · zoology · Body Fluids and Circulation

Frog's heart when taken out of the body continues to beat for some time
Select the best option from the following statements

- **((a))** Frog is a poikilotherm
- **((b))** Frog does not have any coronary circulation
- **((c))** Heart is "myogenic" in nature
- **((d))** Heart is autoexcitable

- **(1)** (c) & (d)
- (2) Only (c)
- (3) Only (d)
- (4) (a) & (b)

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Frog or the vertebrates have myogenic heart having self contractile system or are autoexcitable; because of this condition, it will keep on working outside the body for some time.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The heartbeat starts in specialised muscle (the SA node / sinus venosus pacemaker), so the heart is myogenic and autoexcitable: (c) and (d) explain why it keeps beating once removed. Poikilothermy (a) and coronary circulation (b) do not explain self-generated beating, so the answer is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Regulation of Cardiac Activity · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 6)

![printed question 35](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/crops/q035_c700157e_30102026.png?v=c700157efe25)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **Statements** (`statements`) — lettered statements, the answer is which combination is right
- **Asks:** **Stated reason** (`reason_line`) — a reason NCERT gives: why it happens
- **Concept:** A myogenic heart beats because its own nodal muscle is autoexcitable, firing action potentials with no nerve supply, which is why an excised heart goes on beating
- **Trap:** **Partly correct** (`half_right`) — one part of the option is right and one part is wrong — option 3  
  A student who matches the chapter's own word for the heart, autoexcitable, stops at (d) and leaves (c) aside as vocabulary from elsewhere, but myogenic and autoexcitable name the same property from two sides and the best option takes both.
- **Why:** This question requires the reason behind a stated observation, demonstrated by the stem 'Frog's heart when taken out of the body continues to beat for some time' followed by 'Select the best option from the following statements'.
- **Note:** Statement (b) is defensible as a condition that lets the beat last, which is why option 4 is given a route rather than called filler; the key is tagged as printed, since (c) and (d) are what make the heart beat without innervation.

## Q36

Biology · zoology · Human Health and Disease

Transplantation of tissues/organs fails often due to non-acceptance by the patient's body. Which type of immune-response is responsible for such rejections?

- (2) Autoimmune response
- **(3)** Cell-mediated immune response
- (4) Hormonal immune response

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Non-acceptance or rejection of graft or transplanted tissues/organs is due to cell mediated immune response.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Graft rejection is caused by T-lymphocytes recognising the transplanted tissue as non-self, which is the cell-mediated immune response (CMI), option $3$.

Autoimmunity is defined by the target being the body's own molecules; a graft is non-self, and the response that rejects it is the cell-mediated one.

## Q37

Biology · zoology · Principles of Inheritance and Variation

Thalassemia and sickle cell anemia are caused due to a problem in globin molecule synthesis. Select the correct statement.

- (1) Sickle cell anemia is due to a quantitative problem of globin molecules
- (2) Both are due to a qualitative defect in globin chain synthesis
- (3) Both are due to a quantitative defect in globin chain synthesis
- **(4)** Thalassemia is due to less synthesis of globin molecules

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Thalassemia differs from sickle-cell anaemia in that the former is a quantitative problem of synthesising too few globin molecules while the latter is a qualitative problem of synthesising an incorrectly functioning globin.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Thalassemia is a $\textit{quantitative}$ defect: too few globin chains are made, so $[\text{globin}]_{\text{synthesised}} < [\text{globin}]_{\text{normal}}$.
2. Sickle cell anemia is a $\textit{qualitative}$ defect: a single substitution $\text{Glu} \rightarrow \text{Val}$ at position $6$ of the $\beta$-globin chain gives a globin that does not work properly.
3. So options $1$, $2$ and $3$ are wrong, and option $4$ ("Thalassemia is due to less synthesis of globin molecules") is correct.

**Subtopic:** Thalassemia, Sickle-cell Anaemia · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 6)

![printed question 37](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/crops/q037_6a5662e1_30102026.png?v=6a5662e130cc)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 37](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/solutions/q037_4daa6419_30102026.png?v=4daa6419574b)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **Statements** (`statements`) — lettered statements, the answer is which combination is right
- **Asks:** **Comparison** (`compare`) — the difference between two things, or the correct pairing
- **Concept:** Thalassemia is a quantitative fault — too few normal globin chains are made; sickle-cell anaemia is a qualitative fault — one structurally wrong globin chain
- **Trap:** **Reversed** (`reversed`) — the right thing stated backwards: direction, order or ratio — option 1  
  A student who holds the word pair quantitative/qualitative but not its owners attaches 'quantitative' to the disorder the chapter works out in detail, sickle-cell anaemia. The two labels go the other way: thalassemia makes too few normal chains, sickle-cell anaemia makes one wrong chain.
- **Why:** This question requires separating thalassemia from sickle-cell anaemia on the quantitative-versus-qualitative contrast, demonstrated by options that cross both disorders with both defect types, from 'Both are due to a qualitative defect in globin chain synthesis' to 'Thalassemia is due to less synthesis of globin molecules'.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q38

Biology · zoology · Animal Kingdom

An important characteristic that Hemichordates share with Chordates is

- (1) Pharynx without gill slits
- (2) Absence of notochord
- (3) Ventral tubular nerve cord
- **(4)** Pharynx with gill slits

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Pharyngeal gill slits are present in hemichordates as well as in chordates.
2. Notochord is present in chordates only.
3. Ventral tubular nerve cord is characteristic feature of non-chordates.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Pharyngeal gill slits are a basic chordate character, and Hemichordates also have a pharynx with gill slits; the stomochord is not a true notochord, and a ventral tubular nerve cord is not a chordate feature, so the shared feature is option $4$.

## Q39

Biology · botany · Sexual Reproduction in Flowering Plants

Double fertilization is exhibited by

- **(1)** Angiosperms
- (2) Gymnosperms
- (3) Algae
- (4) Fungi

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Double fertilization is a characteristic feature exhibited by angiosperms.
2. It involves syngamy and triple fusion.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Double fertilization is the fusion of one male gamete with the egg (syngamy) and the other male gamete with the two polar nuclei (triple fusion) within the embryo sac, which is unique to angiosperms, so the answer is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Double Fertilisation · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Double fertilisation — syngamy plus the triple fusion that makes the primary endosperm nucleus — happens only in angiosperms

The gymnosperm nutritive tissue is haploid gametophyte tissue laid down before fertilisation; only in angiosperms does a second male gamete fuse with the polar nuclei.

## Q41

Biology · zoology · Breathing and Exchange of Gases

Lungs are made up of air-filled sacs the alveoli. They do not collapse even after forceful expiration, because of :

- (1) Expiratory Reserve Volume
- **(2)** Residual Volume
- (3) Inspiratory Reserve Volume
- (4) Tidal Volume

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Volume of air present in lungs after forceful expiration as residual volume which prevents the collapsing of alveoli even after forceful expiration.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Residual Volume ($RV \approx 1100$--$1200\,\text{mL}$) is the air that stays in the lungs even after a forcible expiration; this retained air keeps the alveoli from collapsing, which is option $2$.

ERV is a true quantity, but it is the air that LEAVES; what the question asks for is what stays behind, the residual volume of about 1100-1200 mL.

## Q42

Which of the following are not polymeric?

- (2) Nucleic acids
- (3) Proteins
- (4) Polysaccharides

## Q44

Biology · botany · Plant Kingdom

Life cycle of Ectocarpus and Fucus respectively are

- (1) Haplodiplontic, Haplontic
- (2) Haplontic, Diplontic
- (3) Diplontic, Haplodiplontic
- **(4)** Haplodiplontic, Diplontic

1. Ectocarpus shows alternation between a free-living haploid gametophyte and a diploid sporophyte, so its life cycle is $\text{haplodiplontic}$; Fucus has a diploid thallus with gametes as the only haploid stage, so its life cycle is $\text{diplontic}$. The pair (Haplodiplontic, Diplontic) is option $4$.

## Q45

Biology · botany · Ecosystem

Presence of plants arranged into well defined vertical layers depending on their height can be seen best in :

- (1) Temperate Forest
- (2) Tropical Savannah
- **(3)** Tropical Rain Forest
- (4) Grassland

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. The tropical rain forest have five vertical strata on the basis of height of plants. i.e., ground vegetation, shrubs, short canopy trees, tall canopy trees and tall emergent trees.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Vertical stratification means plants of different heights occupy distinct vertical layers (trees, shrubs, herbs); this is shown best in tropical rain forests, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Ecosystem – Structure, Function and Components · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 7)

- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Stratification is sharpest where many species of different heights share one patch, which is the tropical rain forest's emergent, canopy, understorey, shrub and floor profile
- **Trap:** **New trap (proposed)** (`new_trap`) — a way of catching a student that none of the above describes — option 1  
  A student holding the stratification example — trees in the top layer, shrubs below, herbs lowest, in a forest — finds that a temperate forest satisfies every word of that example, and takes the first forest printed. The stem asks where the layering is seen BEST, and the tropical rain forest carries more distinct storeys than any other community.

- **Why:** This question requires the ecosystem whose vertical stratification NCERT treats as the clearest, demonstrated by 'Presence of plants arranged into well defined vertical layers depending on their height can be seen best in :'

## Q46

Biology · botany · Photosynthesis in Higher Plants

Phosphonol pyruvate (PEP) is the primary $\mathrm{CO_2}$ acceptor in :

- (1) $\mathrm{C_3}$ and $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants
- (2) $\mathrm{C_3}$ plants
- **(3)** $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants
- (4) $\mathrm{C_2}$ plants

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. PEP is $3\mathrm{C}$ compound which serves as primary $\mathrm{CO_2}$ acceptor in the mesophyll cell cytoplasm of $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants like maize, sugarcane, Sorghum etc.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. In $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants the primary $\mathrm{CO_2}$ acceptor in mesophyll cells is the 3-carbon PEP, fixed by PEP carboxylase to the 4-carbon OAA; in $\mathrm{C_3}$ plants the primary acceptor is RuBP, so PEP is the primary acceptor in $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** The C₄ Pathway · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 7)

![printed question 46](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/crops/q046_1b5cbf7b_30102026.png?v=1b5cbf7b7fec)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 46](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/solutions/q046_5cd94564_30102026.png?v=5cd945640814)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** In C4 plants the mesophyll fixes CO2 onto PEP to give the four-carbon OAA, while the C3 acceptor is the five-carbon RuBP
- **Trap:** **Partly correct** (`half_right`) — one part of the option is right and one part is wrong — option 1  
  A student who has learnt that C4 plants also run the Calvin cycle reads 'C3 and C4 plants' as the inclusive, safer version of the answer they are sure of. One half holds and the other fails: what C4 plants share with C3 plants is the Calvin cycle, whose acceptor is RuBP, so PEP as the primary acceptor happens nowhere in a C3 plant.
- **Why:** This question requires the photosynthetic group NCERT gives PEP as the primary CO2 acceptor for, demonstrated by 'Phosphonol pyruvate (PEP) is the primary CO2 acceptor in :' with one option bundling two groups.
- **Note:** The stem prints 'Phosphonol pyruvate' for phosphoenolpyruvate; tagged as printed, not corrected.

## Q47

Biology · zoology · Neural Control and Coordination

Good vision depends on adequate intake of carotene rich food
Select the best option from the following statements

- **((a))** Vitamin A derivatives are formed from carotene
- **((b))** The photopigments are embedded in the membrane discs of the inner segment
- **((c))** Retinal is a derivative of vitamin A
- **((d))** Retinal is a light absorbing part of all the visual photopigments

- (1) (b), (c) & (d)
- (2) (a) & (b)
- **(3)** (a), (c) & (d)
- (4) (a) & (c)

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Carotene is the source of retinal which is involved in formation of rhodopsin of rod cells.
2. Retinal, a derivative of vitamin A, is the light-absorbing part of all visual photopigments.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Statement $(a)$: carotene is a provitamin that is converted into vitamin A, so vitamin A derivatives come from carotene. True.
2. Statement $(b)$: the photopigments (opsin + retinal) are in the membrane discs of the outer segment of rods and cones, not the inner segment. False.
3. Statement $(c)$: retinal is the aldehyde of vitamin A, so it is a vitamin A derivative. True.
4. Statement $(d)$: every visual photopigment (rhodopsin and the cone pigments) is opsin plus retinal, and retinal is the part that absorbs light. True.
5. The true statements are $(a)$, $(c)$ and $(d)$, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Sense Organs: The Eye · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 7)

![printed question 47](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/crops/q047_d644273e_30102026.png?v=d644273e28e3)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 47](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/solutions/q047_a022d935_30102026.png?v=a022d935bc52)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **Statements** (`statements`) — lettered statements, the answer is which combination is right
- **Asks:** **Stated reason** (`reason_line`) — a reason NCERT gives: why it happens
- **Concept:** Carotene is the source of vitamin A, and its derivative retinal is the light-absorbing part of every visual photopigment, which is what ties the diet to vision
- **Trap:** **Partly correct** (`half_right`) — one part of the option is right and one part is wrong — option 4  
  A student who verifies (a) and (c), then balks at the sweeping 'all' in (d), stops with the two statements they are sure of, and (d) is correct as printed so the truncated pair loses the third member the key needs.
- **Why:** This question requires the reason behind a stated dependence, demonstrated by the stem 'Good vision depends on adequate intake of carotene rich food' followed by 'Select the best option from the following statements'.

## Q49

Biology · botany · Mineral Nutrition

Select the mismatch :

- (1) Rhizobium – Alfalfa
- (2) Frankia – Alnus
- **(3)** Rhodospirillum – Mycorrhiza
- (4) Anabaena – Nitrogen fixer

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Rhodospirillum is anaerobic, free living nitrogen fixer.
2. Mycorrhiza is a symbiotic relationship between fungi and roots of higher plants.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. $\textit{Rhizobium}$ forms nitrogen-fixing root nodules on legumes such as alfalfa, so option $1$ is a correct match.
2. $\textit{Frankia}$ forms nitrogen-fixing root nodules on the non-leguminous plant $\textit{Alnus}$, so option $2$ is a correct match.
3. $\textit{Anabaena}$ is a cyanobacterium that fixes atmospheric nitrogen, so option $4$ is a correct match.
4. $\textit{Rhodospirillum}$ is a free-living anaerobic nitrogen-fixing bacterium, whereas mycorrhiza is a symbiotic fungus-root association, so option $3$ is the mismatch.

**Subtopic:** Nitrogen Fixation · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 7)

![printed question 49](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/crops/q049_107f8279_30102026.png?v=107f82797d99)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 49](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/solutions/q049_f6fb122b_30102026.png?v=f6fb122be6ec)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **Two answers** (`two_part`) — the answer has two halves, usually joined by 'respectively'
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Nitrogen-fixing partnerships are specific: Rhizobium with legumes, Frankia with the non-legume Alnus, Anabaena fixing on its own; Rhodospirillum is a free-living fixer and forms no mycorrhiza
- **Trap:** **New trap (proposed)** (`new_trap`) — a way of catching a student that none of the above describes — option 2  
  The task asks for the wrong pairing, so a student strikes out the pairings they can check — Rhizobium with alfalfa, Anabaena as a nitrogen fixer — and marks the one left that they cannot check. Frankia with Alnus is printed as a genuine non-legume nitrogen-fixing symbiosis, so the pair that cannot be verified is the one that holds.
- **Proposed:** `unverifiable_true_pair` — in a negated 'select the mismatch' task the option that pulls is the pairing the student cannot check rather than one that is wrong, and this pair is true exactly as printed; answers_other_question needs the option to answer a DIFFERENT question, half_right needs one part of the pair to fail, and nearby_fact needs a wrong fact printed beside the right one
- **Why:** This question requires the partner or role NCERT prints for each named microbe, demonstrated by 'Select the mismatch :' over four organism-partner pairs including 'Rhodospirillum – Mycorrhiza'.
- **Note:** Each option is a pair accepted or rejected whole, which E1 recorded as plain_mcq; recorded here as qtype two_part, since both halves of the printed pair must hold and this axis has no separate value for the codebook's pair_options shape.

## Q50

Biology · botany · Sexual Reproduction in Flowering Plants

Attractants and rewards are required for

- (1) Cleistogamy
- (2) Anemophily
- **(3)** Entomophily
- (4) Hydrophily

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Insect pollinated plants provide rewards as edible pollen grain and nectar as usual rewards.
2. While some plants also provide safe place for deposition of eggs.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Attractants (colour, scent) and rewards (nectar, pollen) are needed only when an animal pollinator must be drawn to the flower; among the options this is insect pollination, entomophily, option $3$, since cleistogamy is self-pollination in closed flowers and anemophily and hydrophily use wind and water.

**Subtopic:** Agents of Pollination · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 7)

![printed question 50](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/30102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_C/crops/q050_34bb4f4c_30102026.png?v=34bb4f4c1690)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Only animal pollination has to be paid for: attractants and rewards exist because an insect must be brought to the flower, while wind and water carry pollen for nothing
- **Trap:** **look alike term** (`look_alike_term`) — two terms RESEMBLE each other and the resemblance is the pull: maturation / meristematic, lyase / ligase, GPP / NPP — option 2  
  Anemophily and entomophily are the same word but for a Greek prefix, and a student who holds the -phily list without having fixed each prefix to its agent takes the wrong one. Anemo- is the wind, which carries pollen without inducement; entomo- is the insect, the visitor that has to be rewarded.
- **Why:** This question requires the pollination mode NCERT attaches floral rewards to, demonstrated by 'Attractants and rewards are required for' standing over three modes that involve no animal visitor.
- **Note:** look_alike_term over nearby_fact by the swap test: replace 'Anemophily' with the plain phrase 'wind pollination' and the pull dies, because the agent is then readable off the option; what carries the student is the shared -phily form with only a prefix between the two terms.

## Q51

Biology · zoology · Reproductive Health

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. NCERT Class 12 (Reproductive Health) says infertility from the male's inability to inseminate or from very low sperm counts is corrected by artificial insemination, with semen placed in the vagina or uterus (IUI), so the answer is option $4$.

## Q52

Biology · zoology · Animal Kingdom

Which among these is the correct combination of aquatic mammals?

- (1) Trygon, Whales, Seals
- (2) Seals, Dolphins, Sharks
- (3) Dolphins, Seals, Trygon
- **(4)** Whales, Dolphins Seals

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

1. Sharks and Trygon (sting ray) are the members of chondrichthyes (cartilaginous fish) while whale, Dolphin and Seals are aquatic mammals belong to class mammalia.

1. Trygon (stingray) and sharks are cartilaginous fishes (Chondrichthyes), not mammals, so options $1$, $2$ and $3$ are out; whales, dolphins and seals are all aquatic mammals, so the answer is option $4$.

## Q54

Root hairs develop from the region of

- **Trap:** **look alike term** (`look_alike_term`) — two terms RESEMBLE each other and the resemblance is the pull: maturation / meristematic, lyase / ligase, GPP / NPP — option 1  
  A student who stores root hairs as belonging to the m-region of the root retrieves meristematic, the other m-named region of the same figure, where cells are still dividing and have grown no hairs.
- **Why:** This question requires the stated fact about which root region bears root hairs, demonstrated by the four options being the four named regions of one root, 'Meristematic activity', 'Maturation', 'Elongation' and 'Root cap'; applying the swap test, the two names in play resemble each other and the codebook names maturation against meristematic as a resemblance pair, so likeness wins over the position of the elongation zone.

## Q55

Biology · botany · Sexual Reproduction in Flowering Plants

A dioecious flowering plant prevents both:

- (1) Cleistogamy and xenogamy
- (2) Autogamy and xenogamy
- **(3)** Autogamy and geitonogamy
- (4) Geitonogamy and xenogamy

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. When unisexual male and female flowers are present on different plants the condition is called dioecious and it prevents both autogamy and geitonogamy.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. A dioecious plant bears male and female flowers on different plants, so each plant has unisexual flowers of only one sex; this prevents both autogamy and geitonogamy while xenogamy still occurs, which is option $3$.
